" data-portal-copyright= " Andr ? e Lanthier " **28;15551;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1741787 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Revolution is in the air in two otherwise wildly disparate productions happening across the city right now . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In &lt;a href= " https : **33;15581;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;The Times They Are A Changin ' &lt;/a&gt; , which originated at Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s Harold Green Jewish Theatre Company and plays at the Segal Centre through March 22 , husband-and-wife team Louise Pitre and W. Joseph Matheson celebrate &amp;rsquo ; 60s protest songs , specifically those written by artists with Jewish heritage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The title clues us in that Bob Dylan will be a significant presence . Indeed , snippets of that eponymous song are threaded throughout the 90-minute show like an increasingly ominous countdown to disenchantment . The accompanying video projections , put together by Dan Bowman , range from kaleidoscopic psychedelia , cheerful images of smiling &amp;rsquo ; 60s personalities and a brave new world of technology , to shocking , iconic photos such as the fleeing &amp;ldquo ; napalm girl&amp;rdquo ; and the summary execution of a Viet Cong member . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pitre , who delivered a towering performance as &amp;Eacute ; dith Piaf in &lt;a href= " https : **33;15616;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;The Angel and the Sparrow&lt;/a&gt; at the Segal , and Matheson , who appeared with her in that 2018 show , find a nice balance between these moods . At first it comes across a bit like a good-natured lounge act , but they soon slip out of formal evening wear and into more comfortable , slightly more countercultural denims . And while the delivery of the songs is initially pretty much easy listening , Pitre shifts up a few gears as the show progresses to really let rip , as in the defiantly feminist You Don&amp;rsquo ; t Own Me and the shattering , gospel-like Save the Country . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Matheson , who wrote the between-song commentaries , understandably leaves the big , spectacular moments to Pitre , often dubbed Canada&amp;rsquo ; s first lady of musical theatre . But he has plenty of enjoyable moments too , particularly the vaudeville-style , deceptively jolly anti-war I-Feel-Like-I&amp;rsquo ; m-Fixin&amp;rsquo ; -to ... Rag . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The show follows the familiar template of the nostalgia-heavy jukebox musical , but Pitre , Matheson and director Avery Saltzman haven&amp;rsquo ; t always gone for the obvious song choices ; several selections had me Googling once I got home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are , however , plenty of crowd-pleasing greatest hits , including The Sound of Silence , One Fine Day and It&amp;rsquo ; s Getting Better . The obligatory cheerfulness of this last song darkens in Pitre&amp;rsquo ; s delivery before she and Matheson segue into the quietly tragic Where Have All the Flowers Gone . It&amp;rsquo ; s a reminder that the decade of flower power and make-love-not-war eventually gave way to the bloody quagmire of Vietnam and conservative retrenchment. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , the team is shrewd enough to send the audience home with a smile , with He Ain&amp;rsquo ; t Heavy , He&amp;rsquo ; s My Brother and an a cappella version of Forever Young ( the five-piece band dropping their instruments to join in ) providing an uplifting climax to this enjoyable , often thought-provoking show . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;AT A GLANCE&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **27;15651;TOOLONG Times They Are A Changin ' &lt;/strong&gt; continues through March 22 at the Segal Centre , 5170 C ? te-Ste-Catherine Rd . Tickets : $67 ; student and under-30 discounts subject to availability . Call 514-739-7944 or visit &lt;a href= " https : **33;15680;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;15715;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; *** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;15746;TOOLONG ... title= " Irina ( Rebecca Vachon , left ) , Olga ( No ? mie Godin-Vigneau ) , Vershinin ( ? ric Bruneau ) , Masha ( Evelyne Brochu ) and Solyony ( Vincent C ? t ? ) look forward , in vain , to better times in Th ? ? tre du Nouveau Monde 's Les 3 Soeurs . " alt= " Irina ( Rebecca Vachon , left ) , Olga ( No ? mie Godin-Vigneau ) , Vershinin ( ? ric Bruneau ) , Masha ( Evelyne Brochu ) and Solyony ( Vincent C ? t ? ) look forward , in vain , to better times in Th ? ? tre du Nouveau Monde 's Les 3 Soeurs . " data-portal-copyright= " Yves Renaud " **28;15781;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1741786 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; As with that progressively dark decade , the party is pooped in Les 3 Soeurs , director Ren&amp;eacute ; Richard Cyr&amp;rsquo ; s surprisingly fleet-footed adaptation of Chekhov&amp;rsquo ; s 1901 classic . It plays at Th&amp;eacute ; &amp;acirc ; tre du Nouveau Monde through March 31 , and has Evelyne Brochu heading the ensemble as the desperately lovestruck Masha. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The curtain opens not on the familiar Chekhovian paraphernalia of steaming samovar and heavy furniture , but on a few hard-back chairs and some garish balloons . It&amp;rsquo ; s the name day of youngest sister Irina ( Rebecca Vachon ) , and the mood is bright with hopes of an exciting move to Moscow . But then the sparklers fizzle and the balloons are burst by viciously misanthropic army captain Solyony ( Vincent C&amp;ocirc ; t&amp;eacute ; ) , and the only change to look forward to is the coming bloodbath of war and revolution that some credit Chekhov with predicting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cyr has ruthlessly pruned the text and shrunk the cast list , bringing the usual three-hours-plus to around 100 minutes . The space is deliberately compressed , too , emphasizing the stifling imprisonment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It makes for a more lively interpretation of Chekhov than we&amp;rsquo ; re perhaps used to . ( There&amp;rsquo ; s also an undertone of comic grotesquery that seems more reminiscent of Gogol . ) It may or may not come as pleasant news that the crushing ennui of the characters&amp;rsquo ; lives is something we&amp;rsquo ; re told about rather than enjoined to experience . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;AT A GLANCE&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **27;15811;TOOLONG 3 Soeurs&lt;/strong&gt; continues through March 31 at Th ? ? tre du Nouveau Monde , 84 Ste-Catherine St. W. Mob brilliantly delivers twist in the tail , punch in the gut at **28;15952;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;15982;TOOLONG ... from Jim **26;16017;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; Jim Burke , Special to Montreal GazetteTue , 10 Mar 2020 13:05:11 **45;16045;TOOLONG ... NewsMontreal theatreReview : Mob brilliantly delivers twist in the tail , punch in the gut at Centaurhttp : **33;16092;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **33;16127;TOOLONG ... title= " Adrianne Richards and Matthew Kabwe keep Mob 's secrets well hidden until the devastating climax . " alt= " Adrianne Richards and Matthew Kabwe keep Mob 's secrets well hidden until the devastating climax . " data-portal-copyright= " Allen McInnis " **28;16162;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1740537 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Early on in Catherine-Anne Toupin&amp;rsquo ; s tough , brilliant thriller Mob , the mysteriously traumatized heroine Sophie vomits on the floor of the remote bed-and-breakfast at which she has just arrived . As Martin , the schlubby nephew of landlady Louise , mops up with a towel , he warns his aunt to back off . &amp;ldquo ; Might be infectious , &amp;rdquo ; he says . That got a big , ever-so-anxious laugh from the first-night audience at Centaur Theatre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In fact , Mob &amp;mdash ; or La Meute , as it was known when it played to sold-out houses at La Licorne &amp;mdash ; isn&amp;rsquo ; t about anything so up-to-the-minute as the current health scare . Yet it is , in its way , about a thoroughly modern disease , the virulent strain of which shows no sign of abating , nor of letting up on its capacity to destroy lives as it spreads far and wide . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Exactly what that destructive force is will have to remain a mystery here . Toupin , who declared herself a fan of both Hitchcock and Harold Pinter in &lt;a href= " https : **33;16192;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;an interview with the Montreal Gazette&lt;/a&gt; , knows how to crank up the menace while keeping a lid on the story&amp;rsquo ; s shocking secrets until the last stretch . She&amp;rsquo ; s a dab hand at shifting moods , too . At one point , as Sophie and Martin share a midnight bottle of hooch , it feels as though the play might be developing into one of those comedy capers about underdogs who have hit on some daft get-rich-quick scheme that might just work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If there are any criticisms to be levelled at this mostly relentless 90-minute three-hander , deftly and abrasively directed by Andrew Shaver and efficiently translated by Chris Campbell , it might be that a central scene of excruciating comic embarrassment plays out a little too long . It might conversely be argued that any impatience for this scene to end can be put down to the fact that it&amp;rsquo ; s so damn uncomfortable . That&amp;rsquo ; s largely down to Matthew Kabwe&amp;rsquo ; s raw , fearlessly self-lacerating , at times heartbreaking performance as the painfully awkward , seemingly inoffensive Martin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;16227;TOOLONG ... title= " Matthew Kabwe gives a raw , fearlessly self-lacerating performance as Martin in Mob . " alt= " Matthew Kabwe gives a raw , fearlessly self-lacerating performance as Martin in Mob . " data-portal-copyright= " Allen McInnis " **28;16262;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1740538 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Adrianne Richards , taking over the role that Toupin herself played at La Licorne , is superb as Sophie , conveying the vertiginous panic of someone whose life has been torn apart , as well as the fury of a woman who refuses to succumb to victimhood . And there&amp;rsquo ; s fine work , too , from Susan Bain as the infirm , good-hearted Louise , who movingly bears witness to the horrors that inevitably unfold in the play&amp;rsquo ; s devastating climax . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;16292;TOOLONG ... title= " Adrianne Richards superbly conveys both vertiginous panic and fury as Sophie in Mob . " alt= " Adrianne Richards superbly conveys both vertiginous panic and fury as Sophie in Mob . " data-portal-copyright= " Allen McInnis " **28;16327;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1740540 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It all takes place in a sparse , prison-like skeleton of a building designed by James Lavoie . Lighting designer Martin Sirois incorporates a bank of lamps at the back of the stage that , at a crucial point , conceals rather than illuminates an explosive moment of ecstasy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As with many hit thrillers , from Sleuth to Deathtrap to the recent Knives Out , Mob unfolds like an elaborate game , but this time one with real-world implications that will possibly leave you reeling , and perhaps a little nauseated , at the hell we humans have managed to create for ourselves . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;AT A GLANCE&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **42;16357;TOOLONG continues through March 29 at Centaur Theatre , 453 St-Fran ? ois-Xavier St. Tickets : $44 to $56 ; under-30s and students $30 ; seniors $40 to $46 . Student rush one hour prior to the show : $18 , upon availability . ArtsLocal NewsMontreal theatreReview : The Lumineers much more than one smash hit at Montreal 's Bell Centrehttp : **34;16720;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **33;16756;TOOLONG ... title= " The Lumineers ' Wesley Schultz at the Bell Centre in Montreal on Friday , March 6 , 2020. " alt= " The Lumineers ' Wesley Schultz at the Bell Centre in Montreal on Friday , March 6 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " Christinne Muschi " **28;16791;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1739903 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; They came with a churchly backdrop , an extravagant visual setup , and a crowd-hugging stage design cribbed from U2&amp;rsquo ; s blueprints . But even with those frills , the Lumineers sounded resolutely no-frills at the Bell Centre on Friday , a mere seven months after &lt;a href= " https : **33;16821;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;their Osheaga headline slot&lt;/a&gt; &amp;mdash ; an intimate U.S. folk-rock band that has made itself at home in arenas without corroding its humble heart . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They&amp;rsquo ; ve picked up a sense of judicious showmanship along the way . Core members Wesley Schultz ( vocals/guitar ) and Jeremiah Fraites ( drums ) appeared on opposite ends of the ringed walkway in a striking entrance , several production budget brackets above a 2013 Metropolis charmer that saw them fighting valiantly to hold a distracted crowd&amp;rsquo ; s attention . Sleep on the Floor&amp;rsquo ; s Springsteen getaway narrative built naturally from Fraites&amp;rsquo ; s steady pulse to a spine-chill resolution , with bassist Byron Isaacs and scene-stealing violinist Lauren Jacobson venturing down from the main stage . It wasn&amp;rsquo ; t the last riveting miniature drama of the night . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With the walkway encircling a general-admission pit and facing another standing-room area , there were multiple front rows for much of the nearly two-hour set . Shortly after Gun Song climaxed with a cathartic singalong and spiralling violin , the big gun was brought out early , as the band skipped back to the centre of the room for Ho Hey . Those exclamations were reduced to hiccups by Bell Centre acoustics , but the sprightly signature song got the carefree performance it required . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;16856;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;16892;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Pacing remains a challenge for a group that doesn&amp;rsquo ; t churn out endless arms-outstretched choruses . An ambling It Wasn&amp;rsquo ; t Easy to Be Happy for You didn&amp;rsquo ; t capitalize on Ho Hey&amp;rsquo ; s momentum , and Slow It Down did just that , with Schultz and Fraites trusting the audience to listen in a room designed for spectacle . To the fans&amp;rsquo ; credit , they did , and were rewarded with Schultz strolling the floor and stands during the wistful Angela , the extremely likeable frontman high-fiving and socializing without an overbearing security battalion at his heels . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The friendly presence was shared by everyone onstage as instruments were casually traded , with old-timey haberdasher Fraites the closest thing to an outsized personality . &amp;ldquo ; He&amp;rsquo ; s Superman , &amp;rdquo ; said Schultz , &amp;ldquo ; because he wears the same thing every day . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;16928;TOOLONG ... title= " The Lumineers ' Jeremiah Fraites at the Bell Centre in Montreal on Friday , March 6 , 2020. " alt= " The Lumineers ' Jeremiah Fraites at the Bell Centre in Montreal on Friday , March 6 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " Christinne Muschi " **28;16963;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1739905 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A galvanizing Gloria was hammered home with roadhouse piano and multiple percussionists , a deceptively joyous soundtrack to the grim domestic-drama visuals accompanying songs from the new album , III . The conceptually linked narratives were played in full , but not in sequence ; even so , the unsettled Jimmy Sparks alone &amp;mdash ; featuring an eerie drone from Jacobson and wonky guitar that **25;16993;TOOLONG have been intentionally out of tune &amp;mdash ; would have been enough to confound anyone who had bought a ticket just to shout &amp;ldquo ; &lt;em&gt;HO ! &lt;/em&gt; &amp;rdquo ; and head home . My Cell was equally daring , the sparse arrangement highlighted by Schultz abandoning his warm rasp for a woozy upper register . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;17020;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;17056;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The breezy , reassuring Cleopatra &amp;mdash ; its Love Minus Zero echo another Dylan homage , after the earlier Subterranean Homesick Blues cover &amp;mdash ; was served up like dessert after a block heavy on III material . Schultz goosed fans into a hell of an ovation , but they seemed primed to oblige anyway . A faithful take on Leonard Cohen&amp;rsquo ; s Democracy originated in a Schultz/Fraites spot at &lt;a href= " https : **33;17092;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;the 2017 Tower of Song tribute concert in the same venue&lt;/a&gt; &amp;mdash ; now a generous full-cast performance including vocal turns from openers J.S. Ondara and Mt . Joy&amp;rsquo ; s Matt Quinn . Given current affairs , having Cohen&amp;rsquo ; s timely words splashed large on the screens was appropriate rather than overbearing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;17127;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;17163;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; What seemed like occasional problematic pacing earlier in the night came off as uncompromising when the impressively sombre Salt and the Sea was delivered as the penultimate number , just before Schultz offered the full house a final boisterous chant in the all-together-now winner Stubborn Love . Long before then , the Lumineers had confirmed they have the range , confidence and smarts to remain in arenas on their terms , years after enjoying a freak smash hit that no longer defines them . Even the confetti blast capping Big Parade felt organic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **28;17199;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **35;17229;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **26;17266;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **32;17294;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; Jordan Zivitz , Montreal GazetteSun , 08 Mar 2020 02:51:27 **45;17328;TOOLONG ... ArtsLocal NewsMusicAge against the machine : How to make older Montrealers feel welcome at the partyhttp : **35;17375;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **33;17412;TOOLONG ... title= " If you 've found yourself in the middle of an Osheaga audience , perhaps you 'll agree that young people are taller than they used to be . How about more seating for those who are n't up for straining their neck ? " alt= " If you 've found yourself in the middle of an Osheaga audience , perhaps you 'll agree that young people are taller than they used to be . How about more seating for those who are n't up for straining their neck ? " data-portal-copyright= " John Kenney " **28;17447;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1738808 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;17477;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;It 's a truism , but that does n't mean it is n't true : Montrealers place a premium on having a good time , and we have evolved a culture to facilitate that proclivity . But is the big party equally accessible to all ? Is the demographic often referred to as " of a certain age " -- for convenience 's sake , let 's define it as those born during or prior to the Diefenbaker administration -- being subtly shunted out ? ( The recent &lt;a href= " https : **33;17513;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Nuit Blanche&lt;/a&gt; is an outlier here , with any practical challenges more than forgivable in light of the unique concept. ) &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;It 's not a matter of wanting to dictate the terms . Going to all-night reggae bills used to be one of the great joys of this boomer 's life , but now I just ca n't do it , and I 've made my peace with it . No , it 's more a question of having some basic needs acknowledged . We like to get a good night 's sleep . We like to have the option of sitting down once in a while . Is that so much to ask ? Herewith , then , some suggestions to those who run the show . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3 class= " p1 " &gt;&lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Start on **28;17548;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;It 's hard not to feel there 's some sort of class-based bias at play here . When you go to , say , the opera , you can be secure in knowing the show will start at the advertised time , or very close to it . So why is it acceptable for small-venue alternative music events to start egregiously late , and without so much as an onstage apology ? ( Yes , indie rock , I 'm looking mostly at you here . ) As my mother would say to me when I showed up late for family dinners , that 's just bad manners . Moreover , it 's a practice that serves as built-in discouragement to those elders among us who might still feel inclined to investigate the scene now and then . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3 class= " p1 " &gt;&lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Do n't have multiple opening **28;17578;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;It 's only asking for trouble . Informal studies have shown that with each additional act on a music bill , the likelihood of delay increases exponentially . One opener is plenty . This would be a trade-off , granted -- you might miss out on seeing tomorrow 's &lt;a href= " https : **33;17608;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Billie Eilish&lt;/a&gt; today . But the reclaimed sleep will be worth it . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3 class= " p1 " &gt;&lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Do n't make us **29;17643;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;There 's more to this than wanting ( and sometimes needing ) to sit . I ca n't back it up with hard statistics , but anecdotally I 'm here to say that young people are taller than young people used to be . As an average-height person formerly accustomed to standing in the middle of a crowd at a general-admission non-seated venue and having a clear view of the stage , I find that to attempt the same thing now is to feel lost in an old-growth forest . As for staking out a space at the front , the last time I was willing to do that was for Kraftwerk at Metropolis in 2014 , and that was because they were Kraftwerk . Soberingly , I recently found that even leaning against a counter for three-plus hours -- at a Vampire Weekend show in the same venue , the balcony seats being full and the floor being crammed with tall young people -- was exhausting . Solution : more seats . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;17674;TOOLONG ... title= " If you 've found yourself in the middle of an Osheaga audience , perhaps you 'll agree that young people are taller than they used to be . How about more seating for those who are n't up for straining their neck ? " alt= " If you 've found yourself in the middle of an Osheaga audience , perhaps you 'll agree that young people are taller than they used to be . How about more seating for those who are n't up for straining their neck ? " data-portal-copyright= " John Kenney " **28;17709;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1738808 " /&gt; &lt;h3 class= " p1 " &gt;&lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Finish at a reasonable **28;17739;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;This is , of course , related to the first item above , though it 's not inseparable . Nothing can put a damper on your enjoyment of a cultural event quite like the lingering thought that you might miss the last m ? tro home and be saddled with a cab or Uber fare equal to or greater than the price of the ticket . When I lived in N.D.G. and attended many shows in Mile End , the problem grew to the point where eventually I threw up my hands and stayed home . See also : the babysitter factor . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3 class= " p1 " &gt;&lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Turn down the canned music at large-scale sporting events &lt;/span&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Better yet , turn it all the way off . The Montreal Canadiens have been around for a long time , and they have cultivated a sophisticated fan base that does n't need to be bullied into cheering . And when we do cheer , it would be nice to be able to hear ourselves doing it . As indeed it would generally be nice to converse with the person in the next seat without screaming . The situation is less acute at Alouettes games , mostly because there is no roof on Molson Stadium . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3 class= " p1 " &gt;&lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Expand the washroom facilities at larger venues &lt;/span&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;It 's a question of ratio . Take the Bell Centre ( again ) . It has what certainly appear to be commodious washrooms , but in practical terms&lt;span class= " Apple-converted-space " &gt; &lt;/span&gt;we know perfectly well that everyone 's going to want to use them at the same time , and anyone who has attended a Habs game has seen what that means . It would be nice to do what one has to do without the risk of missing the first five minutes of the next period . Hey , future planners , let 's err on the side of too many , not too few . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;17769;TOOLONG ... title= " There 's a welcoming atmosphere at the very informal Tam-Tams , unless nature calls . " alt= " There 's a welcoming atmosphere at the very informal Tam-Tams , unless nature calls . " data-portal-copyright= " Dave Sidaway " **28;17804;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1738831 " /&gt; &lt;h3 class= " p1 " &gt;&lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Acknowledge reality at the **32;17834;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;One needs to tread carefully here , because &lt;a href= " https : **33;17868;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;the Tam-Tams ' &lt;/a&gt; beauty is inseparable from the fact that the event arose organically . The super-informal chill-out polyrhythmic gathering every summer Sunday under the gaze of Sir George- ? tienne Cartier has never needed corporate branding or mandatory wristbands or nanny-state supervision of any kind . But allow me to suggest that it would not constitute commercial accommodation to provide the thousands with a few discreetly placed portaloos . We of a certain age would be especially appreciative . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3 class= " p1 " &gt;&lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Bring back the Mount Royal Funicular Railway &lt;/span&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Life in our city offers few pastimes as sublime as a stroll up Olmsted Trail on a pleasant day . That said , the final push to the Belvedere can be taxing even for the heartier among us . How about an optional funicular at roughly the halfway point , perhaps alongside the Grand Staircase ? Everybody loves a funicular -- even just saying the word out loud is fun . An old-timey one , like the model last used at Mount Royal in 1918 , would be an attraction in itself . But this is the first time he has come to the Montreal area with the Disney series , and he couldn&amp;rsquo ; t be more excited . He says he has around 150 friends and family members coming to the show over the weekend . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I have almost my whole entire skating club coming , people from the school I used to go to ( Marymount Academy in N.D.G. ) and my mom &amp;hellip ; and my sister has never gotten to see the show before . " alt= " Charlotte Legault is a fluently bilingual actress who made her name in the franco world of television with her recurring role on the hit series District 31 . She is now appearing in the CTV series Transplant . She grew up in Pointe-Claire Village and is the fourth generation of Legaults to live in the same home . " data-portal-copyright= " Peter McCabe " **28;19212;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1735824 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Actor Charlotte Legault&amp;rsquo ; s world is lived in both official languages . She was a familiar face for fans of Radio-Canada&amp;rsquo ; s hit daily television show District 31 for 110 episodes over three seasons and is now appearing as Dr. Theo Hunter&amp;rsquo ; s wife Melissa on the medical drama Transplant , which debuted on CTV and VRAK last week . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Getting a couple of serious television series under one&amp;rsquo ; s belt is often enough to encourage a move to Toronto , New York , Paris or L.A. But not for Legault . Not yet . A &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;t the end of each gig , she returns to her home in Pointe-Claire Village . She is the fourth generation of Legaults to live in the family home . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My great-grandfather built the home , &amp;rdquo ; Legault said . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve lived in the village my whole life . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The role of Melissa is &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Legault 's first recurring role in a big Canadian series . The show is &lt;/span&gt; produced by Sphere Media Plus and created by executive producer and show-runner Joseph Kay . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Transplant&amp;rsquo ; s central character is Syrian doctor Bashir Hamed , played by Hamza Haq . The action swirls around the medical and personal challenges encountered by emergency staff at a busy Toronto hospital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;19242;TOOLONG ... title= " A scene from the CTV medical drama Transplant . Pictured are Charlotte Legault , in her role as Melissa , interacting with her TV family . Melissa is married to Dr. Theo Hunter , played by Jim Watson . ( Yan Turcotte/ courtesy of RuGicomm ) . " alt= " A scene from the CTV medical drama Transplant . Pictured are Charlotte Legault , in her role as Melissa , interacting with her TV family . Melissa is married to Dr. Theo Hunter , played by Jim Watson . ( Yan Turcotte/ courtesy of RuGicomm ) . " data-portal-copyright= " YanTurcotte " **28;19277;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1735837 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Transplant is new territory for Legault. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s the first time I&amp;rsquo ; ve played a doctor&amp;rsquo ; s wife . And we have two kids &amp;mdash ; which I don&amp;rsquo ; t have , &amp;rdquo ; Legault said . Her &amp;ldquo ; husband&amp;rdquo ; is played by Jim Watson ( Mary Kills People ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Jim is fabulous to work with , &amp;rdquo ; Legault said . &amp;ldquo ; The show has a good balance of action , drama , tension and romance . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Legault is what&amp;rsquo ; s called &amp;ldquo ; a triple threat&amp;rdquo ; , which means she sings , dances and acts . Over the last 14 years , she has weathered hundreds of auditions . There was a good deal of rejection . It&amp;rsquo ; s the nature of the business . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; After 14 years , it&amp;rsquo ; s hard not to take it personally . You really put yourself out there , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; I am so lucky to have my parents . They encourage me . Just when I&amp;rsquo ; m thinking it&amp;rsquo ; s time to stop , something comes along . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Acting was not Legault&amp;rsquo ; s first choice . She spent nine years training intensively in artistic gymnastics . The demands of the training resulted in her attending three elementary schools and two high schools . She needed to be close to her training facility and to be enrolled in a school with a Sport &amp;Eacute ; lite program which allowed athletes to train during the day as well as attend academic classes . At her peak , she was juggling academics with up to 35 hours a week of training , with competitions every weekend . But her goal of competing in the Olympics was not in the cards , so she stopped . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was very depressed , &amp;rdquo ; Legault said . &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s when my parents suggested I take ballet , singing and acting classes . I got so busy , I didn&amp;rsquo ; t have time to think back . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She landed her first lead role at 16 , in the 2007 Rafael Ouellet film Derri&amp;egrave ; re Moi which debuted at the Toronto International Film Festival and went on to tour the international film circuit . Then came a string of teen roles . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; At 23 , I was still playing a 15 year old , &amp;rdquo ; Legault said . &amp;ldquo ; I was scared I would get stuck doing only teen series . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Her luck shifted when she was hired for one episode of the hit detective series District 31 . Over three seasons , she kept getting called back to do more , resulting in a best supporting actress nomination at the Gala G&amp;eacute ; meaux. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; " I ended up doing 110 episodes , " Legault said . " That 's huge for a one-episode contract . We would shoot four episodes in five days . It was good training . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These days , in between acting assignments , Legault spends her downtime working on getting her Private Pilot&amp;rsquo ; s License ( PPL ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What&amp;rsquo ; s next on the acting front ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Lots of auditions and some call backs , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A shot at a Broadway musical would be great . She just finished directing a short video , which got her thinking about learning more about directing . &amp;nbsp ; And she has a supporting role in the short film The Maids will come on Monday &amp;ndash ; about a dysfunctional family gathering for Christmas dinner &amp;ndash ; directed by Harley Chamandy and premiering at the Manchester Film Festival this month . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Transplant airs on CTV , Wednesday at 9 p.m. and , dubbed in French , on VRAK at 10 p.m. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **31;19307;TOOLONG " **38;19340;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;19380;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;19422;TOOLONG ... season of love -- eccentric , unmitigated , sour and soul -- is coming to **27;19457;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;19486;TOOLONG ... to headline 16th edition of Strangers in the Night **25;19521;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;19548;TOOLONG ... looking for artists to paint mural in **28;19583;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; Kathryn Greenaway , Montreal GazetteWed , 04 Mar 2020 13:05:07 **45;19613;TOOLONG ... Island GazetteNuit Blanche is all about making art accessible , all night longhttp : **36;19660;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **33;19698;TOOLONG ... title= " Thousands of revellers packed the area around Place des Arts for the 2012 edition of Nuit Blanche . " People here like to party , especially by the end of February , " says Laurent Saulnier , vice-president of programming at L ' ? quipe Spectra , which organizes the all-nighter. " alt= " Thousands of revellers packed the area around Place des Arts for the 2012 edition of Nuit Blanche . " People here like to party , especially by the end of February , " says Laurent Saulnier , vice-president of programming at L ' ? quipe Spectra , which organizes the all-nighter. " data-portal-copyright= " Tim Snow " **28;19733;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1733682 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;19763;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nuit Blanche is a concept tailor-made for Montreal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Back in 1989 , the Helsinki Festival kicked off something called Night of the Arts , a similar idea in which folks could check out museums , art galleries and bookstores in a single night . Nuit Blanche came to life in 2002 in Paris , and once again the focus was on contemporary art . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nuit Blanche began here in 2003 , and organizers gave it a unique Montreal twist . Like events in Helsinki , Paris and some 120 other cities around the world , our Nuit Blanche has a contemporary art component , but there is much more to the all-night event here . It features a wide array of cultural genres , notably music , virtual-reality installations and standup comedy . Because it&amp;rsquo ; s Montreal , the bottom line is that it&amp;rsquo ; s one big party &amp;mdash ; and better yet , almost everything is free . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We really adapted La Nuit Blanche to the spirit of Montreal , &amp;rdquo ; said Laurent Saulnier , vice-president of programming at L&amp;rsquo ; &amp;Eacute ; quipe Spectra , which organizes the event . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The spirit of Montreal is all about partying , let&amp;rsquo ; s admit it , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; People here like to party , especially by the end of February , when it&amp;rsquo ; s been a couple of months of days getting dark too early , Arctic cold , etc. , etc . People want to go out . So we give people the perfect excuse to go out &amp;mdash ; a wide variety of activities in all sorts of different places , and the vast majority of them are free of charge . It&amp;rsquo ; s pretty cool . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This year&amp;rsquo ; s Nuit Blanche takes control of the city centre Saturday night into Sunday morning . You can sample an astonishing amount of culture &amp;mdash ; everything from electronic-music DJs Tokimonsta and Whipped Cream at Place des Festivals to Canadian paintings from the 1980s at the Mus&amp;eacute ; e d&amp;rsquo ; art contemporain , a virtual-reality adaptation of Franz Kafka&amp;rsquo ; s terrifying novella The Metamorphosis at the Goethe-Institut , and drag queens performing out on the street in the Gay Village . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; La Nuit Blanche is for me a very big , very open menu from which you can choose whatever tempts you , &amp;rdquo ; said Saulnier . &amp;ldquo ; There are loads of people , for example , who use La Nuit Blanche to move their bodies , because there are places where you can skate and do other sports . For others , La Nuit Blanche is all about the joy of going to a museum at midnight and discovering the museum at an unusual time of day . For some , La Nuit Blanche is simply synonymous with the word &amp;lsquo ; party . &amp;rsquo ; &amp;hellip ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a menu where absolutely everything is possible . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;19799;TOOLONG ... title= " Nuit Blanche 's outdoor site featured a dance floor in 2015 . Events this year range from museum visits to DJ sets and virtual-reality installations . " alt= " Nuit Blanche 's outdoor site featured a dance floor in 2015 . Events this year range from museum visits to DJ sets and virtual-reality installations . " data-portal-copyright= " Peter McCabe " **28;19834;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1733681 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Roughly 300,000 people participate in Nuit Blanche each year . By way of comparison , a huge day at the Montreal International Jazz Festival attracts maybe 100,000 to 150,000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We think that it&amp;rsquo ; s the day of the year when there&amp;rsquo ; s the biggest number of people who go out , where there&amp;rsquo ; s more people than on any other day who have decided to do something other than sitting at home watching Netflix , &amp;rdquo ; said Saulnier . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nuit Blanche is also spreading across the city more and more . The first edition happened only in Old Montreal and downtown . This year , the party is hopping downtown ( &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;including the underground world of the Art Souterrain festival ) and in&lt;/span&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;the Quartier Latin , the Gay Village , Hochelaga , Plateau-Mont-Royal , Mile End , Montreal North and Old Montreal . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Whatever the neighbourhood , the philosophy remains the same : it&amp;rsquo ; s about making culture accessible to everyone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Whatever you like , I&amp;rsquo ; m convinced that you&amp;rsquo ; ll find at least one activity that will appeal to you , &amp;rdquo ; said Saulnier . &amp;ldquo ; But on Nuit Blanche , why not go do something you don&amp;rsquo ; t normally do ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That might mean going to the Canadian Centre for Architecture ; checking out DJs and immersive installations at the Phi Centre ; watching some of the best TV commercials from the past year as part of Les Lions de Cannes 2019 , playing at Th&amp;eacute ; &amp;acirc ; tre Outremont until 3 in the morning ; sampling art videos in the Mus&amp;eacute ; e d&amp;rsquo ; art contemporain exhibit Points of Light ; or watching performances by drag queens Sasha Baga , Wendy Warhol and Ayzisse Baga on Ste-Catherine St. near the corner of Atateken St. in the heart of the Gay Village . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The 12th edition of the Art Souterrain festival kicks off the same night and fits in perfectly with Nuit Blanche , given that both are dedicated to making the arts more accessible . The festival showcases art in underground settings , including Complexe Guy-Favreau , the Palais des congr ? s , the World Trade Centre and Place Victoria . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We always launch the night of Nuit Blanche , &amp;rdquo ; said &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Art Souterrain founder and director general Fr ? d ? ric Loury . " We kind of take advantage of this winter event that 's built around free events . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of the centrepieces of the festival this year is what they&amp;rsquo ; re calling a Giant Escape Game &amp;mdash ; a kind of Journey to the Center of the Earth treasure hunt in which participants try to figure out puzzles as they travel through the underground city . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People will be asked to answer questions about the works of art they see , and so it&amp;rsquo ; s like a treasure hunt but built around contemporary art , &amp;rdquo ; said Loury. &amp;ldquo ; There are no prizes at the end . It&amp;rsquo ; s all about the pleasure of participating , and it&amp;rsquo ; s for people who maybe don&amp;rsquo ; t go to art exhibitions that often , so they can appreciate works of art and maybe better understand the backstory around these works of art . They&amp;rsquo ; ll be like investigators , trying to discover the truth behind the works of art . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;AT A GLANCE&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **28;19864;TOOLONG Blanche&lt;/strong&gt; takes place the evening of Saturday , Feb. 29 through the early hours of Sunday , March 1 , presented as part of &lt;strong&gt;the Montr ? al en lumi ? re festival . &lt;/strong&gt; For more information , including programming details , see &lt;a href= " https : **25;19894;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **32;19921;TOOLONG ... or &lt;a href= " https : **28;19955;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;19985;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **27;20018;TOOLONG **25;20047;TOOLONG opens Saturday , Feb. 29 and continues through Sunday , March 22 . " " data-portal-copyright= " Zoran Jelenic " **28;20553;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1734199 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; As Bette Davis almost said : fasten your seatbelts , Montreal , it&amp;rsquo ; s going to be a bumpy night when legendary drag troupe Les Ballets Trockadero de Monte Carlo lands at Place des Arts for a one-night stand on Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The divine Miss Davis is just one larger-than-life influence cited by the company&amp;rsquo ; s artistic director , Tory Dobrin , when he spoke to the Montreal Gazette by phone . As well as Joan Crawford and Bolshoi giant Maya Plisetskaya , he mentioned such high-energy comedians as Lucille Ball , Phyllis Diller , Gracie Allen and Joan Rivers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I send videos to the guys and I say , &amp;lsquo ; Please look at the way they use their eyes and their facial expressions , and even the way they tip their heads , &amp;rsquo ; &amp;rdquo ; said Dobrin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Trocks , as they&amp;rsquo ; re affectionately known , were launched in New York in 1974 , part of a more assertive push for LGBTQ visibility in the after-effects of the Stonewall riots . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What began as a late-night lark in off-off-Broadway venues soon blossomed into a truly spectacular hothouse flower . They&amp;rsquo ; ve performed with Shirley MacLaine , had think pieces written about them in publications such as the New Yorker and New York Times , and were snapped for a photo essay in Vogue . Now an international brand , they were the subject of the film Rebels on Pointe , which was nominated in a documentary category at the 2019 Canadian Screen Awards . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re almost 50 years old now , which kind of speaks for itself , &amp;rdquo ; said Dobrin , who joined as a dancer in 1980. &amp;ldquo ; We have staying power . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gender politics have obviously undergone major changes since the Trocks first jet&amp;eacute ; d onto the scene , but Dobrin argues the troupe is still very much a progressive force . &amp;ldquo ; When you talk about drag and its place in society , a lot of times it&amp;rsquo ; s used to highlight the absurdities and inconsistencies of gender stereotypes . We&amp;rsquo ; ve definitely always played with that . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As an example , Dobrin points to the Trocks&amp;rsquo ; treatment of Swan Lake . &amp;ldquo ; Usually the Swan Queen is very vulnerable , while the prince is all strong and macho . We do it the other way around . We play the prince as being some sort of insignificant , possibly clueless man , and the Swan Queen as a very powerful character running the show . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By way of highlighting the excellence of the dancers , Dobrin cited several award-winners in the company , including Cuban-born Carlos Hopuy , who will be one of the dancers performing in Montreal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Carlos has won gold medals as a male in international ballet competitions . He&amp;rsquo ; s top of the line . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s not only the promise of first-class dancers that pulls genuine ballet enthusiasts into the Trocks&amp;rsquo ; fan base . It&amp;rsquo ; s also the company&amp;rsquo ; s loving guardianship of traditional ballet , both old favourites and the less familiar . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ve developed a large repertory of ( Russian ) ballets that aren&amp;rsquo ; t really done in the West , &amp;rdquo ; said Dobrin. &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s garnered some attention from critics who actually wanted to see these ballets and never had the opportunity . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dobrin mentioned The Little Humpbacked Horse , first presented at St. Petersburg&amp;rsquo ; s Imperial Ballet in 1864 , as well as Walpurgisnacht , a short piece that derives from Gounod&amp;rsquo ; s Faust . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; No one does Walpurgisnacht outside of Russia anymore , &amp;rdquo ; Dobrin said , with more than a hint of pride . &amp;ldquo ; So the audience in Montreal will see this old Bolshoi ballet . Of course , we&amp;rsquo ; ve changed it around to bring out the comedy in it , but at the same time ( the Montreal audience ) is going to be exposed to the kind of vocabulary that was important to that ballet . They&amp;rsquo ; re going to see some sophisticated ballet stuff . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;AT A GLANCE&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **27;20583;TOOLONG Ballets Trockadero de Monte Carlo&lt;/strong&gt; perform Thursday , March 5 at Th ? ? tre Maisonneuve of Place des Arts . ArtsLocal NewsMontreal theatreTheatre : Hit thriller La Meute becomes a snarling Mob at Centaurhttp : **33;21411;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **33;21446;TOOLONG ... title= " While the dynamic plotting of Mob is full of carefully calibrated surprises , playwright Catherine-Anne Toupin acknowledges the main theme : " We 're all becoming desensitized to a lack of empathy and understanding for somebody who 's different . " " alt= " While the dynamic plotting of Mob is full of carefully calibrated surprises , playwright Catherine-Anne Toupin acknowledges the main theme : " We 're all becoming desensitized to a lack of empathy and understanding for somebody who 's different . " " data-portal-copyright= " John Mahoney " **28;21481;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1734127 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Playwright Catherine-Anne Toupin likes to make her audiences gasp with shock and awe . She has heard those gratifying gasps many times over the last two years while performing in her own twisting , turning thriller La Meute , which opens at the Centaur on Tuesday in an English version called Mob . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Every single night , there were two moments especially when I heard people saying &amp;lsquo ; oh my god ! &amp;rsquo ; very loudly , &amp;rdquo ; recalled Toupin. &amp;ldquo ; Which I love . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; La Meute was first produced in 2018 at La Licorne , which is where Toupin joined the Montreal Gazette for a chat . Such was the enormous buzz the play created that it was reprised twice , both times selling out well in advance of opening night . Centaur artistic director Eda Holmes was at the premi&amp;egrave ; re , immediately collared Toupin as she came off stage shaking with nervous energy , and offered there and then to put it on at the Centaur . The English version&amp;rsquo ; s run has already been extended by a week due to popular demand . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No doubt part of the attraction of the play has been the dynamic plotting , which sees a woman named Sophie taking to the road after a work-related trauma . She winds up at a remote bed-and-breakfast run by a deceptively nice young man and his elderly relative . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Already you might have the screeching violins of a certain movie thriller playing in your head , and you wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be far off the mark . Although Toupin says the similarities in those opening scenes to the plot of Psycho are entirely accidental , she fesses up to being steeped in the language , mood and hairpin twists of classic edge-of-the-seat films . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was introduced to them by my wonderful grandma , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; She loved old movies and she was the official babysitter to me and my sister , and we only watched old movies together . So that&amp;rsquo ; s where I discovered the likes of Hitchcock and Otto Preminger . I was probably 12 when I saw Psycho for the first time &amp;mdash ; too young ! But I really enjoyed it . So Hitchcock especially has , I think without my knowing it , had a huge influence on my writing . I&amp;rsquo ; ve seen all his movies at least 20 times . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Exactly where Mob goes after that intriguing opening is something we can&amp;rsquo ; t discuss here if we&amp;rsquo ; re to preserve its carefully calibrated surprises . Suffice to say the play&amp;rsquo ; s themes revolve around misogyny in the workplace . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think what&amp;rsquo ; s relevant is the violence between the characters , &amp;rdquo ; said Toupin , with practised avoidance of spilling too many beans . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s really about how they get into that vicious circle and can&amp;rsquo ; t get themselves out of it . I think we&amp;rsquo ; re all becoming desensitized to a lack of empathy and understanding for somebody who&amp;rsquo ; s different . That&amp;rsquo ; s the main subject of the play . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The misogyny angle has clearly struck a chord in the #MeToo era , and the exact nature of&amp;nbsp ; Sophie&amp;rsquo ; s workplace ( we have to keep that under wraps , too ) is arguably central to the powerful effect of the play . But at the end of the day , said Toupin , &amp;ldquo ; to me it has no importance which industry this play takes place in . It could take place in the film industry , it could take place in a restaurant . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ( For the record , the play has nothing to do with the Qu&amp;eacute ; b&amp;eacute ; cois far-right group that goes by the name La Meute . Toupin began writing it well before that ugly development in the province&amp;rsquo ; s politics. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;21511;TOOLONG ... title= " Catherine-Anne Toupin at La Licorne , where her play La Meute -- about trauma and misogyny , set in a remote B&amp;B -- sold out three times over . " alt= " Catherine-Anne Toupin at La Licorne , where her play La Meute -- about trauma and misogyny , set in a remote B&amp;B -- sold out three times over . " data-portal-copyright= " John Mahoney " **28;21546;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1734126 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; As well as the promise of a gripping night out , La Meute had another ace up its sleeve when it played La Licorne : the onstage appearance of Toupin herself . She&amp;rsquo ; s a household name among francophone Quebecers , thanks to her television appearances in the prison drama Unit&amp;eacute ; 9 , Les hauts et les bas de Sophie Paquin and in the sitcom Boomerang , which she also created . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Born in 1975 in Ottawa , where her father worked in the federal government , Toupin soon moved to Quebec City after her father got a job in the provincial government . She came to Montreal in 1996 when she enrolled at the Conservatoire de musique et d&amp;rsquo ; art dramatique , after which she started Th&amp;eacute ; &amp;acirc ; tre ni plus ni moins with Fr&amp;eacute ; d&amp;eacute ; ric Blanchette and Fran&amp;ccedil ; ois L&amp;eacute ; tourneau , both now major figures on Montreal&amp;rsquo ; s francophone theatre scene . That company&amp;rsquo ; s first production was Toupin&amp;rsquo ; s L&amp;rsquo ; Envie , which she also acted in , and which played at Th&amp;eacute ; &amp;acirc ; tre d&amp;rsquo ; Aujourd&amp;rsquo ; hui in 2004. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Toupin cites Harold Pinter as one of her favourite playwrights , and you can see his influence in her second play , &amp;Agrave ; pr&amp;eacute ; sent , which premi&amp;egrave ; red at La Licorne in 2007 . It&amp;rsquo ; s a superb tragicomic domestic nightmare with distinct similarities to Pinter&amp;rsquo ; s The Homecoming . Unaccountably , no anglo theatre in Montreal , or indeed in Canada , has picked it up &amp;mdash ; it had to travel to Bath in the U.K. to play in English , as Right Now . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All the francophone first runs of Toupin&amp;rsquo ; s plays have seen her taking the lead female role . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s important for me , when I&amp;rsquo ; ve written something , to perform it for the first time , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; I find that&amp;rsquo ; s part of the arc of creation . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She speaks flawless English , so I wondered aloud why she isn&amp;rsquo ; t again putting herself into Sophie&amp;rsquo ; s shoes for this Centaur production . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Oh , I&amp;rsquo ; ve already performed it 80 times , &amp;rdquo ; she laughed , with a dismissive wave , before expressing confidence in the play&amp;rsquo ; s new Sophie , Adrianne Richards , as well as with Richards&amp;rsquo ; s co-stars , Matthew Kabwe and Susan Bain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As with Right Now , this English version is by Chris Campbell , a British translator who was literary manager in London&amp;rsquo ; s prestigious Royal Court Theatre . Toupin reckons director Andrew Shaver will keep the play grounded in a Canadian milieu . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Of seeing her work make the leap across the linguistic divide , Toupin said : &amp;ldquo ; I actually find translation exciting . You lose something , but gain something else . I think the biggest challenge in the English translation has been to find the poetry of the violence . &amp;hellip ; It feels like a kind of a dream place that the characters enter , and I want the audience to enter into that too &amp;mdash ; to be appalled , but also strangely compelled . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And , of course , to yell &amp;ldquo ; oh my god ! &amp;rdquo ; in all the right places . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;AT A GLANCE&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **42;21576;TOOLONG plays from Tuesday , March 3 through Sunday , March 29 at Centaur Theatre , 453 S&lt;/em&gt; &lt;em&gt;t-Fran ? ois-Xavier St. Tickets : $44 to $56 ; under-30s and students $30 ; seniors $40 to $46 . Student rush one hour prior to show : $18 , upon availability . ArtsLocal NewsMontreal theatreDunlevy in Berlin : St ? phane Dion is here for the party , and much morehttp : **36;22008;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **33;22046;TOOLONG ... title= " " I am not aware of any diplomat from any country unhappy to be in Berlin , " says St ? phane Dion ( pictured in 2016 ) , Canada 's ambassador to Germany and special envoy to the European Union . " alt= " " I am not aware of any diplomat from any country unhappy to be in Berlin , " says St ? phane Dion ( pictured in 2016 ) , Canada 's ambassador to Germany and special envoy to the European Union . " data-portal-copyright= " FLORENCE CASSISI " **28;22081;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1733302 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; BERLIN &amp;mdash ; St&amp;eacute ; phane Dion threw a party in Berlin last Saturday . Hundreds of people came . There was free beer , wine and hors d&amp;rsquo ; oeuvres . He told jokes , which went over as well as St&amp;eacute ; phane Dion jokes can . The DJ played Canadian pop music , including Shania Twain and Len . ( Remember Len ? ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Well , OK , the Canadian Embassy threw the party . But as Canada&amp;rsquo ; s ambassador to Germany and special envoy to the European Union , that made it Dion&amp;rsquo ; s party . And though Dion is not exactly a party guy , he was rolling with it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Based out of Germany&amp;rsquo ; s hippest city , the former leader of the Liberal Party is enjoying life after partisan politics . And he&amp;rsquo ; s enjoying Berlin &amp;mdash ; perhaps not as you or I might enjoy Berlin , but he&amp;rsquo ; s enjoying it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I am not aware of any diplomat from any country unhappy to be in Berlin , &amp;rdquo ; Dion said in an interview at the Canadian Embassy on Monday morning . &amp;ldquo ; The city itself is very enjoyable . It&amp;rsquo ; s still affordable . Traffic is a bit difficult , but if you compare it even with Munich , it&amp;rsquo ; s much better . The cuisine &amp;mdash ; I will not make any faux pas about German cuisine , but it&amp;rsquo ; s universal here . You have all the styles of restaurant you want . There are more museums than you&amp;rsquo ; ll have time to visit . And a lot of green spaces . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Green spaces are important to Dion , a renowned environmentalist and former environment minister . His dog Kyoto , now 14 , is &amp;ldquo ; in better shape than the protocol of the same name , &amp;rdquo ; he said wryly . ( Again with the jokes ! ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On that note , Saturday&amp;rsquo ; s party was a celebration of this year&amp;rsquo ; s substantial Canadian presence at the Berlinale ( a.k.a. the Berlin International Film Festival ) . Dion missed the opening film , Quebec director Philippe Falardeau&amp;rsquo ; s My Salinger Year , the Thursday before , as he was a guest at a climate change conference in London . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think my name is following me everywhere I go , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t think a traditional ambassador would do that &amp;mdash ; it&amp;rsquo ; s more St&amp;eacute ; phane Dion . I have different hats . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One hat he will be wearing more often this year is that of Canadian culture promoter . Canada is the guest of honour at the 2020 Frankfurt Book Fair , held in October . It&amp;rsquo ; s one of the biggest events of its kind , with 7,500 exhibitors , 10,000 journalists and 300,000 visitors representing 109 countries . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On top of the 90 Canadian publishers and 80 authors who will be present at the fair , approximately 360 Canadian cultural and literary events are planned around Germany through the end of the year , with support from the Canada Council for the Arts and the Government of Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Artists from various disciplines are coming through , from musicians Rufus Wainwright , Tanya Tagaq , Richard Reed Parry and Kid Koala to filmmakers Atom Egoyan and Jennifer Baichwal , graphic novelist Julie Doucet , author Rawi Hage , documentary theatre company Porte Parole , dancer Clara Furey and Victor Quijada&amp;rsquo ; s Rubberband dance company . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian Embassy is involved in various ways in facilitating and promoting these events . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are working in partnership with the Canada Council , &amp;rdquo ; Dion said . &amp;ldquo ; We have knowledge on the ground , with all the institutions here . Between the two , with all the different cultural programming selected , it&amp;rsquo ; s really formidable . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;22111;TOOLONG ... title= " Prime Minister Justin Trudeau and Quebec author Kim Th ? y talk about migration and diversity at the Munich Security Conference this month . " It was a beautiful discussion between the two , and the Germans were fascinated , " says St ? phane Dion . " alt= " Prime Minister Justin Trudeau and Quebec author Kim Th ? y talk about migration and diversity at the Munich Security Conference this month . " It was a beautiful discussion between the two , and the Germans were fascinated , " says St ? phane Dion . " data-portal-copyright= " Canadian Embassy " **28;22146;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1733310 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Those thinking all this is a little outside Dion&amp;rsquo ; s wheelhouse may be forgetting that when he was leader of the opposition , he was also the spokesperson for the Liberals on heritage and culture . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Seeing Canada accept the invitation from the Frankfurt Book Fair brings him satisfaction , as he watched the Harper government decline the same opportunity in 2014 &amp;mdash ; a decision he criticized at the time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I said , &amp;lsquo ; We need to say the book fair is something we will support&amp;rsquo ; &amp;mdash ; and once elected , we did it . Yes , it&amp;rsquo ; s an investment of money , but it&amp;rsquo ; s nothing compared to what it gives Canada in terms of visibility and business opportunities . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Far from being a separate entity a world apart from politics , the arts can open doors , according to Dion , and bring cultures together in ways traditional diplomacy can&amp;rsquo ; t. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the recent Munich Security Conference , Quebec author Kim Th&amp;uacute ; y was invited to sit down for a one-hour public discussion with Prime Minister Justin Trudeau . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Munich conference had proposed a discussion between Trudeau and a Canadian author , in recognition of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s position as the guest of honour at the Frankfurt Book Fair . Dion initially considered more academic authors , but when he was informed it should be a fiction writer , Th&amp;uacute ; y came to mind . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When thinking about security , one issue ( internationally ) is refugees , &amp;rdquo ; he said . Th&amp;uacute ; y and her family came to Canada as refugees in 1979 , when she was a child . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It was a beautiful discussion between the two , and the Germans were fascinated , &amp;rdquo ; Dion said . &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s exactly what we need to do . That&amp;rsquo ; s cultural diplomacy at its best &amp;mdash ; a book fair and a security conference . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Sunday , Dion took in a screening of Quebec director &lt;a href= " https : **33;22176;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Ana ? s Barbeau-Lavalette 's La d ? esse des mouches ? feu&lt;/a&gt; . The expressionistic , sex- and drug-fuelled coming-of-age story had its world premi&amp;egrave ; re in the Berlinale&amp;rsquo ; s Generation 14plus section . So how did a straight-shooter like Dion react to such a wild cinematic ride ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I found it very interesting , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; It was very interesting to see this world where young people are struggling with drugs , trying to find a way , and the adults are trying to help them , but not always with the right approach . These are problems we have everywhere , in Germany as well . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And then , being an experienced diplomat , he found a way to bring it back to politics . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I liked it because it&amp;rsquo ; s something we as parents and teens may remember . It&amp;rsquo ; s an issue our government decided to handle differently , in terms of marijuana . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dion , as you may have noticed , is a trouper . For a man who had quit politics just a few years ago , after being shuffled out of his role as Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s minister of foreign affairs in 2017 , he has not only found his way back &amp;mdash ; he is enjoying himself . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m not in partisan politics anymore , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m working for all Canadians . The truth is , I feel very close to the prime minister as his special envoy ( to the European Union ) . I work for him and for my country . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As long as I feel I am useful for my country , I feel well . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;22211;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;22242;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **25;22280;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **31;22307;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;22340;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22382;TOOLONG ... in Berlin : Quebec TV series gets international **29;22417;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22448;TOOLONG ... in Berlin : Ana ? s Barbeau-Lavalette 's rock ' n ' roll high **27;22483;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22512;TOOLONG ... in Berlin : Philippe Falardeau misses his own **26;22547;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22575;TOOLONG ... in Berlin : Philippe Falardeau squints into film fest **30;22610;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22642;TOOLONG ... we take Berlin : Quebec filmmakers swarm **30;22677;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; T'Cha Dunlevy , Montreal GazetteThu , 27 Feb 2020 20:19:43 **45;22709;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " Bertrand Calmeau " **28;23547;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1732229 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; BERLIN &amp;mdash ; The duo of Fran&amp;ccedil ; ois L&amp;eacute ; tourneau and Jean-Fran&amp;ccedil ; ois Rivard are no longer Quebec&amp;rsquo ; s best-kept secret . The creators of the hit TV series Les Invincibles and &lt;a href= " https : **33;23577;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;S ? rie noire&lt;/a&gt; premi&amp;egrave ; red their new show C&amp;rsquo ; est comme &amp;ccedil ; a que je t&amp;rsquo ; aime ( Happily Married ) Tuesday evening at the 70th Berlinale &amp;mdash ; a.k.a. the Berlin International Film Festival &amp;mdash ; where they are in select company . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In its sixth year , the Berlinale Series section highlights just eight TV and streaming programs from around the globe , with adventurous new works by Cate Blanchett , &lt;a href= " https : **33;23612;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Damien Chazelle&lt;/a&gt; , Jason Segel and &lt;a href= " https : **33;23647;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Greece 's Athina Rachel Tsangari&lt;/a&gt; , among others . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As we said last night , &amp;lsquo ; Qui l&amp;rsquo ; e&amp;ucirc ; t cru ? &amp;rsquo ; ( Who would have thought ? ) , &amp;rdquo ; said director Rivard. &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a nice pat on the back . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In TV , there are no premi&amp;egrave ; res , &amp;rdquo ; added writer-actor L&amp;eacute ; tourneau. &amp;ldquo ; This is validation . It&amp;rsquo ; s good for the ego &amp;hellip ; ( and ) it&amp;rsquo ; s an interesting spotlight . We had no international ambitions , but if the show can get outside of Quebec and intrigue people , great . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Imbued with the same quirkiness , visual sophistication and deadpan humour of their previous productions , C&amp;rsquo ; est comme &amp;ccedil ; a que je t&amp;rsquo ; aime follows the wildly unpredictable antics of two couples &amp;mdash ; Huguette and Ga&amp;eacute ; tan , Serge and Micheline &amp;mdash ; in the Quebec City suburb of Ste-Foy in 1974 . Their relationships are thrown into disarray when their kids go off to summer camp for three weeks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;23682;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;23717;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The first two episodes of the series were shown at the Berlinale . A shrewd bit of foreshadowing at the beginning of the first episode offers a teaser as to where the seemingly innocuous plot might be headed : the two couples are found floating naked , face down in a backyard pool , and are subsequently described as &amp;ldquo ; the worst criminals in the history of organized crime in the Quebec City region&amp;rdquo ; in the &amp;rsquo ; 70s . It&amp;rsquo ; s more funny than scary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We like the unpredictable , &amp;rdquo ; Rivard said . &amp;ldquo ; When we write a story , we try to give audiences something they don&amp;rsquo ; t expect . If you know what&amp;rsquo ; s coming , it gets boring fast . We like to keep people destabilized . It motivates you to keep watching . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The offbeat , incongruous aspect makes it so that if you just watched the first and the 10th episode , you would be like , &amp;lsquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not the same series , &amp;rsquo ; &amp;rdquo ; L&amp;eacute ; tourneau said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Not that it&amp;rsquo ; s not the same series , &amp;rdquo ; Rivard countered , &amp;ldquo ; but you will have missed some things . A lot happens . Each episode has its peculiarities , and leads to the next , like dominoes . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That ability to surprise has made L&amp;eacute ; tourneau and Rivard&amp;rsquo ; s projects stand out on the Quebec television landscape , and is now bringing them around the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Berlinale Series&amp;rsquo ; new section director , Julia Fidel , was impressed by the self-contained universe the pair created , and the diverse cultural comparisons that can be made . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The show &amp;ldquo ; constructs an entire world of its own with black humour and 1970s interiors , &amp;rdquo ; she says in &lt;a href= " https : **33;23753;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;an interview published on the Berlinale website&lt;/a&gt; . &amp;ldquo ; It reminds me of John Updike&amp;rsquo ; s novel Couples . A colleague in the selection committee thought of the Coen brothers , and I have had a long conversation with the writer about parallels with The Ice Storm by Ang Lee . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We find ourselves in a very special microcosm in the series &amp;mdash ; a purely adult world in which boredom leads to some very surprising twists and turns . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although L&amp;eacute ; tourneau and Rivard wrote their previous shows together , L&amp;eacute ; tourneau scripted C&amp;rsquo ; est comme &amp;ccedil ; a que je t&amp;rsquo ; aime alone . He was inspired by events in his personal life &amp;mdash ; some from his childhood , and some from the more recent past . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My girlfriend and I brought our kid to summer camp , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; He was eight years old , and we were nervous to be without him . Having a kid changes things in a relationship &amp;mdash ; I came back from summer camp when I was 10 years old and my parents told me they were separating . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I didn&amp;rsquo ; t want to write the series alone , but I had this idea . &amp;hellip ; I wanted to go back to the theme of relationships we had started with Les Invincibles , and of crime we explored in S&amp;eacute ; rie noire . It&amp;rsquo ; s as if the elements from both series were combined : thesis , antithesis , synthesis . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; L&amp;eacute ; tourneau and Rivard are tickled that their little show is part of one of the most respected film festivals in the world , heralded as one of Europe&amp;rsquo ; s big three alongside Cannes and Venice . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s the kind of international attention that Quebec cinema has been enjoying for some time , and that they believe Quebec television could aspire to more often with the right support . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have to invest in TV and streaming even more , &amp;rdquo ; Rivard said . &amp;ldquo ; We have incredible talent in Quebec , but people are doing shows for $500,000 . How do you compete with the Swedes on that ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have the material , but we have to get beyond the tendency to sit back and ( bask in the accomplishments of ) C&amp;eacute ; line Dion , Cirque du Soleil and Arcade Fire . We have the experience now with TV , which surpasses cinema in terms of people&amp;rsquo ; s interest . That&amp;rsquo ; s the feeling in the industry . This series was amazing to make , but it can be very tough . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;AT A GLANCE&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **27;23788;TOOLONG first two episodes of C'est comme ? a que je t'aime&lt;/strong&gt; screen at a launch event on Thursday , March 5 at 7:30 p.m. at the Cin ? math ? que qu ? b ? coise , 335 de Maisonneuve Blvd . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;24402;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1729663 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Loverboy will headline the 16th edition of the Strangers in the Night gala set for June 20 in Pierrefonds. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian rock band rose to stardom in the 1980s with a string of hits like &amp;ldquo ; &lt;em&gt;Turn Me Loose&lt;/em&gt; &amp;rdquo ; , &amp;ldquo ; &lt;em&gt;The Kid is Hot Tonight&lt;/em&gt; &amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; &lt;em&gt;Working for the Weekend&lt;/em&gt; . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Event promoter Larry Day said he&amp;rsquo ; s been trying to get Loverboy to play at Strangers in the Night for years , but their schedules never meshed. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This year , Day moved up the event&amp;rsquo ; s usual date , from August to June , to accommodate the band . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Opening for Loverboy will be Montreal&amp;rsquo ; s Shane Murphy . Other musical groups appearing include Persuasion , a Santana tribute band , Undercover Legends of Rock , Atomic Glamour , &amp;nbsp ; the TruthWalker , a Michael Jackson impersonator. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Day sad the event has grown since its inception in 2005 when it was held at the former Firm Club on Hymus Blvd , near Sources Blvd. , in Dorval. &amp;ldquo ; It was held as a first anniversary party for the Firm , &amp;rdquo ; Day said last Thursday at a press conference at the &lt;a href= " https : **33;24432;TOOLONG ... Then we did it again the next year , and again till year four when there was a traffic jam from Hymus across Sources Blvd. , up to the Trans-Canada , just to get to the&amp;nbsp ; event . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We just outgrew it , but Fairview was nice to come on board after that , and we did nine years at Fairview . ( Pierrefonds-Roxboro ) Mayor Jim Beis didn&amp;rsquo ; t want us to move the event out of the West island so he was nice enough to arrange for us to use the area on Pierrefonds Blvd. for the past three years . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The event now attracts over 4,500 patrons to the giant parking lot next to Pierrefonds-Roxboro borough hall ( on the site of the former Pierrefonds Arena ) with gourmet dining areas spread under 65,000 square feet of tenting. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Strangers in the Night &amp;ldquo ; gourmet gala charity&amp;rdquo ; &amp;nbsp ; features food from over 80 restaurants , and &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Day always manages to bring in a popular music headliner. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Past editions have featured The Supergroup Styx , Boy George and Culture Club , Kool &amp;amp ; the Gang , Blue Rodeo , KC and Sunshine Band , The Pointer Sisters , Simple Plan , Sheila E and April Wine . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another key part of the event is that it also serves as a major fundraiser for several children&amp;rsquo ; s charities . Over 15 years , Day said the event has raised $6 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This year&amp;rsquo ; s charities are &lt;a href= " https : //www.asmfmh.org/en/ " &gt;Friends of Mental Health&lt;/a&gt; , Lymphoma Canada and Cure SMA ( Spinal Muscular Atrophy ) Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Day said the annual show has become a true community event . &amp;ldquo ; Everyone comes together . The restaurants donate all their food . We get breaks on all the equipment .
